) such that for
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
:

\displaystyle J(u_{1},u_{2})\;=\;\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]+

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}\sum_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2})V(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}})\,.

(22)

Now for any belief
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
such that
s_{1}=(\mathit{loc}_{1},\mathit{per}_{1})
, action
a_{1}\in A_{1}
, agent state
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
and stage strategy
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
, letting
P_{1}\triangleq P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})
by (
21
) we have:

\displaystyle V(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}})\;=\;\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\langle\alpha,(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}})\rangle

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!=\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{E}^{\prime})\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}

rearranging

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!=\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\frac{P((s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})}{P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})}\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}

by (
7
)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!=\frac{1}{P_{1}}\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})P((s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}\!\!

rearranging

\displaystyle\;=\frac{1}{P_{1}}\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\big(\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}},s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}b_{1}(s_{E})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}\in A_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\textup{d}s_{E}\big)\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}

by (
9
)

\displaystyle\;=\frac{1}{P_{1}}\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\big(\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\big(\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}\in A_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}\big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

rearranging.

(23)

Next, for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}(S)
,
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
,
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
we let
\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be the function where for any
s=((\mathit{loc}_{1},\mathit{per}_{1}),s_{E})\in S
:

\displaystyle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s)

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s)\delta(s,(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\textup{d}s_{E}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s)\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta(s,(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

(24)

and the summation in
s_{E}^{\prime}
is due to the finite branching of
\delta
.
Combining (
23
) and (
24
) we have:

\displaystyle V(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P_{1}}\sup\nolimits_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1},s_{E})b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})}\sup\nolimits_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

(25)

by definition of
P_{1}
.
Substituting (
25
) into (
22
), the payoff function
J(u_{1},u_{2})
equals:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})V(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\mbox{$\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle\,.

(26)

We next show that the von Neumann’s Minimax Theorem
[
27
]
applies to the game
\llbracket{\mathsf{C}}\rrbracket
with the payoff function
J
and strategy spaces
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
. This theorem requires that
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
are compact convex sets (which is straightforward to show) and that
J
is a continuous function that is concave-convex, i.e.,

•

J(\cdot,u_{2})
is concave for fixed
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
;

•

J(u_{1},\cdot)
is convex for fixed
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
.

By Definition
3
the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]
can be rewritten as:

\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}b_{1}(s_{E})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))\textup{d}s_{E}

and thus,
\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]
is bilinear in
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
, and thus concave in
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and convex in
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
.

We next show that
u_{1}(a_{1})\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle
is continuous and concave in
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and convex in
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
. The continuity and concavity in
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
follows directly as it is linear in
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
. For
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
, we consider the function
f(u_{2})=\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle
. By (
24
) we have that
f(u_{2})
equals:

\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

and therefore
f(u_{2})
is linear in
u_{2}
. Since the point-wise maximum over linear functions is continuous and convex, it follows that
\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}f(u_{2})
is continuous and convex in
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
, and hence
u_{1}(a_{1})\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle
is continuous and convex in
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
. According to von Neumann’s Minimax theorem:

\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}J(u_{1},u_{2})=\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}J(u_{1},u_{2})\end{array}

and hence the equality between (
18
) and (
19
) holds.

Next we prove the equality of (
18
) and (
20
).
Letting
\textup{Conv}(\Gamma)
be the convex hull of
\Gamma
,
recall that
\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
is the set of vectors of functions in
\textup{Conv}(\Gamma)
indexed by the elements of
A_{1}\times S_{1}
. The function
J(u_{1},u_{2})
in (
26
) can be rewritten as follows:

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}$}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle\Big)

(27)

where
\bar{\alpha}=(\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}})_{a_{1}\in A_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}
, and given
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
, the supremum over
\Gamma
only depends on
a_{1}
and
s_{1}^{\prime}
and using the same arguments as
[
19
, Proposition 4.11]
we have:

\begin{array}[]{c}\sup_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\langle\alpha,(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle=\sup_{\alpha\in\textup{Conv}(\Gamma)}\langle\alpha,(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle\end{array}

for
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
. We next define the game with strategy spaces
\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
and payoff function
J_{u_{1}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}\times\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)\to\mathbb{R}
where for
\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
and
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
:

\displaystyle J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},u_{2})=\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\langle\alpha^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1},u_{2}}[r(s,a)]+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\big(\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}\in A_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})

\displaystyle\cdot\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

by (
24
).

(28)

Substituting (
27
) and (
28
) into (
18
) we have:

\displaystyle\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}J(u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad=\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}$}J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},u_{2})\,.

(29)

We next show that Sion’s Minimax Theorem
[
32
]
applies to the game with strategy spaces
\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
and payoff function
J_{u_{1}}
. Sion’s Minimax Theorem requires that:

•

\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
is convex;

•

\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
is compact and convex;

•

for any
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
the function
J_{u_{1}}(\cdot,u_{2})\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is upper semicontinuous and quasi-concave;

•

for any
\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
the function
J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},\cdot)\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
is lower semicontinuous and quasi-convex.

The first properties clearly hold and the second to follow from (
28
) which demonstrate that both
J_{u_{1}}(\cdot,u_{2})
and
J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},\cdot)
are linear.

Therefore using Sion’s Minimax Theorem, we have:

\begin{array}[]{c}\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},u_{2})=\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},u_{2})\end{array}

and combining with (
29
) it follows that
\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\min\nolimits_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}J(u_{1},u_{2})
equals:

\displaystyle\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\min\nolimits_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}J_{u_{1}}(\overline{\alpha},u_{2})

\displaystyle=\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\min\nolimits_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}+\beta\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\Big(\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\Big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

by (
28
)

\displaystyle=\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\min\nolimits_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}\mbox{$\sum_{a_{2}}$}u_{2}(a_{2}\mid s_{1},s_{E})

\displaystyle\quad\Big(\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})

\displaystyle\quad\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\Big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

rearranging

\displaystyle=\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\min\nolimits_{a_{2}\in A_{2}}\Big(\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\mbox{$\sum_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\Big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

since
\mathsf{Ag}_{2}
is fully informed

\displaystyle=\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\big(\min\nolimits_{a_{2}\in A_{2}}f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}(s_{1},s_{E})\big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

by (
2
)

\displaystyle=\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\sup\nolimits_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}\langle f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

by Definition
4

which demonstrates that (
18
) and (
20
) are equal, i.e.,
T
and
T_{\Gamma}
are equivalent.

Fixed point.
To show the unique fixed point of
T
and
T_{\Gamma}
is
V^{\star}
.
We first prove that
V^{\star}
is a fixed point of the operator
T
, i.e.,
V^{\star}=[TV^{\star}]
. According to the proof of Theorem
4.1
, for
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
the value function
V^{\star}
can be represented by:

\displaystyle V^{\star}(s_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}}V_{\sigma_{1}}(s_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}}\mbox{$\int\nolimits_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}b_{1}(s_{E})\big(\inf\nolimits_{\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},s_{E})}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}[Y]\big)\textup{d}s_{E}

by (
15
)

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}}\langle\inf\nolimits_{\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},s_{E})}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}[Y],(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\langle\alpha,(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

where
\Gamma\triangleq\{\inf\nolimits_{\sigma_{2}\in\Sigma_{2}}\mathbb{E}_{(s_{1},s_{E})}^{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}[Y]\mid\sigma_{1}\in\Sigma_{1}\}
. According to the operator equivalence above, we have:

[TV^{\star}](s_{1},b_{1})=\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}$}\langle f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

(30)

for all
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
, where
\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}\triangleq\{\{\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\}_{a_{1}\in A_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}\mid\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\in\textup{Conv}(\Gamma)\}
and
\Gamma
is given above. Now, by following the same argument as in the proof of
[
19
, Lemma 6.7]
, we can show that
V^{\star}(s_{1},b_{1})=[TV^{\star}](s_{1},b_{1})
for all
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
, i.e.,
V^{\star}=[TV^{\star}]
.

Next we demonstrate that the operator
T
is a contraction mapping on the space
\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
with respect to the supremum norm
\|J\|=\sup_{(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}}|J(s_{1},b_{1})|
. Therefore consider any
J_{1},J_{2}\in\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
and for any belief
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
, let
(u_{1}^{1\star},u_{2}^{1\star})
and
(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{2\star})
be the minimax strategy profiles in the stage games
[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})
and
[TJ_{2}](s_{1},b_{1})
, respectively. Also, let
\bar{J}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})
and
\bar{J}_{2}(u_{1},u_{2})
be the values of state
(s_{1},b_{1})
of the stage game under the strategy pair
(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})\times\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
when computing the backup values in (
22
) for
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, respectively. Without loss of generality, we assume
[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})\leq[TJ_{2}](s_{1},b_{1})
, and thus since
(u_{1}^{1\star},u_{2}^{1\star})
is minimax strategy profile for
[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})
:

\displaystyle\bar{J}_{1}(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})

\displaystyle\leq\bar{J}_{1}(u_{1}^{1\star},u_{2}^{1\star})

\displaystyle=[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})

by definition of
\bar{J}_{1}

\displaystyle\leq[TJ_{2}](s_{1},b_{1})

without loss of generality

\displaystyle=\bar{J}_{2}(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{2\star})

by definition of
\bar{J}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\bar{J}_{2}(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})

\displaystyle\mbox{since $(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{2\star})$ is minimax strategy}.

(31)

Now using (
31
) for any
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
we have

\displaystyle|[TJ_{2}](s_{1},b_{1})-[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})|\leq\bar{J}_{2}(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})-\bar{J}_{1}(u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})

\displaystyle=\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})\big(J_{2}(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2}^{1\star},s_{1}^{\prime}})-J_{1}(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2}^{1\star},s_{1}^{\prime}})\big)

by (
22
)

\displaystyle\leq\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})\|J_{2}-J_{1}\|

by definition of
\|\cdot\|

\displaystyle=\beta\|J_{2}-J_{1}\|

since
P(\cdot\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),u_{1}^{2\star},u_{2}^{1\star})
is a distribution.

(32)

Now by definition of the supremum norm:

\displaystyle\|[TJ_{2}]-[TJ_{1}]\|

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}}|[TJ_{2}](s_{1},b_{1})-[TJ_{1}](s_{1},b_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq\sup\nolimits_{(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}}\beta\|J_{2}-J_{1}\|

by (
32
)

\displaystyle=\beta\|J_{2}-J_{1}\|

rearranging

and hence, since
\beta\in(0,1)
, we have that
T
is a contraction mapping. Thus, the fact that the value function
V^{\star}
is the unique fixed point of

now follows directly from Banach’s fixed point theorem.

Lemma 5
(PWC function)

For any
a\in A
,
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{F}_{C}(S)
, if
\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S\to\mathbb{R}
is the function where for any
s\in S
:

\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}(s)=\mbox{$\sum_{(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{s}^{a}}$}\delta(s,a)(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

then
\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}
is PWC.

Proof

Let
a=(a_{1},a_{2})
. Since
\alpha
is PWC, there exists an FCP
\Phi
of
S
such that
\alpha
is constant in each region of
\Phi
.
According to Assumption
1
,
there exists a pre-image FCP
\Phi^{\prime}
of
\Phi+\Phi_{P}
for joint action
a
, where
\Phi_{P}
is the perception FCP for
\mathsf{Ag}_{1}
. Consider any region
\phi^{\prime}\in\Phi^{\prime}
and let
\phi
be any region of
\Phi+\Phi_{P}
such that
\Theta_{s}^{a}\cap\phi\neq\varnothing
for all
s\in\phi^{\prime}
. Since
\Phi_{P}
is the perception FCP for
\mathsf{Ag}_{1}
, there exists
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
such that if
s^{\prime}\in\phi
, then
s^{\prime}=(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})
for some
s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}
and let
\phi_{E}=\{s_{E}\in S_{E}\mid(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E})\in\phi\}
. If
s,\tilde{s}\in\phi^{\prime}
such that
s=(s_{1},s_{E})
and
\tilde{s}=(\tilde{s}_{1},\tilde{s}_{E})
, then using Assumption
1
we have
\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\Theta_{s}^{a}\cap\phi}\delta(s,a)(s^{\prime})=\sum_{\tilde{s}^{\prime}\in\Theta_{\tilde{s}}^{a}\cap\phi}\delta(\tilde{s},a)(\tilde{s}^{\prime})
and
s_{1}=\tilde{s}_{1}
. Now combining this fact with Definition
2
, it follows that:

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{s}^{a},s_{E}^{\prime}\in\phi_{E}}$}\delta(s,a)(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})=\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{\tilde{s}}^{a},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime}\in\phi_{E}}$}\delta(\tilde{s},a)(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})\,.

Since
\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})=\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})
for any
(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime}),(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})\in\phi
and
S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}=\{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}\mid\mathit{obs}_{1}(\mathit{loc}_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})=\mathit{per}_{1}^{\prime}\}
is equal to
\{\phi_{E}\mid\phi\in\Phi^{s_{1}^{\prime}}\}
for some finite set of regions
\Phi^{s_{1}^{\prime}}\subseteq\Phi+\Phi_{P}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{s}^{a},s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}}$}\delta(s,a)(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})\in\Theta_{\tilde{s}}^{a},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{s_{1}^{\prime}}}$}\delta(\tilde{s},a)(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},\tilde{s}_{E}^{\prime})

and therefore
\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}(s)=\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}(\tilde{s})
, implying that
\alpha^{a,s_{1}^{\prime}}
is constant in each region of
\Phi^{\prime}
.

Lemma 6
(LP for minimax and P-PWLC, extended Lemma
1
)

If
V\in\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
is P-PWLC with PWC
\alpha
-functions
\Gamma
, for any
(s_{1},b_{1})\in S_{B}
,
[TV](s_{1},b_{1})
is given by the LP over the real-valued variables
(v_{\phi})_{\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}}
,
(\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}})_{(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1},\alpha\in\Gamma}
and
(p^{a_{1}})_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\mbox{\rm maximise}\;\;\mbox{$\sum_{\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}}$}v_{\phi}\mbox{$\int_{(s_{1},s_{E})\in\phi}$}b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}\;\;\mbox{\rm subject to}

\displaystyle v_{\phi}\leq\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}p^{a_{1}}r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lambda^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}\geq 0,

\displaystyle p^{a_{1}}{=}\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}p^{a_{1}}{=}1

(33)

for all
\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}
,
a_{2}\in A_{2}
,
(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}
and
\alpha\in\Gamma
where
s_{E}\in\phi
. Moreover, if
(\overline{v}^{\star},\overline{\lambda}_{1}^{\star},\overline{p}_{1}^{\star})
is the optimal solution to the LP (
33
), then the maximiser of the maxsup operator in Definition
4
is
(\overline{p}_{1}^{\star},\overline{\alpha}^{\star})
, where
\overline{\alpha}^{\star}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
is such that for
(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}
, if
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and
p^{\star a_{1}}>0
, then
\alpha^{\star a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}(\lambda^{\star a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}_{\alpha}/p^{\star a_{1}})\alpha

and
\alpha^{\star a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s)=L
for all
s\in S
otherwise.

Proof

Since
V
is P-PWLC, then according to Definitions
4
and
6
and Theorem
4.2
:

\displaystyle[TV](s_{1},b_{1})=\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}$}\langle f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\sup_{\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}}$}\mbox{$\int_{s_{E}\in S_{E}}$}\big(\mbox{$\min_{a_{2}}$}f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}(s_{1},s_{E})\big)b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

(34)

which can be formulated as the following optimization problem:

\displaystyle[TV](s_{1},b_{1})=

\displaystyle\max\nolimits_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1}),\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}},\overline{v}}\mbox{$\sum_{\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}}$}v_{\phi}\mbox{$\int_{(s_{1},s_{E})\in\phi}$}b_{1}(s_{E})\textup{d}s_{E}

\displaystyle\textup{ subject to }v_{\phi}\leq f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}(s_{1},s_{E})\quad\mbox{for all $\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}$ and $a_{2}\in A_{2}$}

where
\overline{v}=(v_{\phi})_{\phi\in\Phi_{\Gamma}}
,
f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}
is constant over
\phi
and
(s_{1},s_{E})\in\phi
.
Using (
2
), the constraint
v_{\phi}\leq f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}(s_{1},s_{E})
can be written as:

\displaystyle v_{\phi}\leq

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))

\displaystyle+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1},s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime}).

Since
\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\in\textup{Conv}(\Gamma)
,
we have
\alpha^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\alpha
for some vector of real-values
(\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}})_{(a_{1},s_{1})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}}
such that
\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=1
, and therefore:

\displaystyle v_{\phi}

\displaystyle\leq\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}u_{1}(a_{1})r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1},s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}

\displaystyle\quad\;\;u_{1}(a_{1})\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}p_{a_{1}}r((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))+

\displaystyle\quad\;\;+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1},s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}}$}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}\alpha(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

where
p_{a_{1}}=u_{1}(a_{1})
for all
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and in the equality we scale
\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=p_{a_{1}}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
for all
a_{1}\in A_{1}
,
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
and
\alpha\in\Gamma
, which gives the constraints:

\displaystyle\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;\geq 0

\displaystyle p_{a_{1}}

\displaystyle=\;\mbox{$\sum_{\alpha\in\Gamma}$}\lambda_{\alpha}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}p_{a_{1}}

\displaystyle=\;1

and hence the fact we can solve the LP problem (
33
) to compute
[TV](s_{1},b_{1})
follows directly.

Proof
(Proof of Theorem
5.1
)

P-PWLC closure.
Consider the LP in Lemma
1
, i.e., (
33
) in the extended Lemma
6
, which computes the minimax or maxsup backup
[TV](s_{1},b_{1})
when
V
is P-PWLC. The polytope of feasible solutions of the LP defined by the constraints is independent of the environment belief
b_{1}
, because
b_{1}
only appears in the objective. Therefore, the set
Q_{s_{1}}
of vertices of this polytope is also independent of
b_{1}
. For each
b_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(S_{E})
, the optimal value of an LP representing
[TV](s_{1},b_{1})
can be found with the vertices
Q_{s_{1}}
, as the objective is linear in
\hat{V}
for any given
b_{1}
. There is a finite number of vertices
q\in Q_{s_{1}}
, and each vertex
q\in Q_{s_{1}}
corresponds to some assignment of variables
u_{1}^{q}
and
\overline{\alpha}^{q}
(
u_{1}^{q}
and
\overline{\alpha}^{q}
are computed by (
33
)).
Since
Q_{s_{1}}
is finite, then letting
Q=\{q\in Q_{s_{1}}\mid s_{1}\in S_{1}\}
, which is finite, we have:

\displaystyle[TV](s_{1},b_{1})=\mbox{$\max_{q\in Q}$}\langle f_{u_{1}^{q},\overline{\alpha}^{q}},(s_{1},b_{1})\rangle\,.

Moreover, since
f_{u_{1},\overline{\alpha},a_{2}}
is PWC for any
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1}),\overline{\alpha}\in\Gamma^{A_{1}\times S_{1}}
and
a_{2}\in A_{2}
, then it follows from Definition
4
, the function
f_{u_{1}^{p},\overline{\alpha}^{p}}
is PWC. This implies that
[TV]\in\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
and P-PWLC.

Convergence.
Using the fixed point in Theorem
4.2
, the conclusion directly follows from Banach’s fixed point theorem and the fact we have proved in Theorem
5.1
that if
V\in\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
and P-PWLC, so is
[TV]
.

Proof
(Proof of Lemma
2
)

By following the proof of Theorem
5.1
and how
\overline{p}_{1}^{\star}
and
\overline{\alpha}^{\star}
are constructed, we can easily verify that in Algorithm
1

\alpha^{\star}
is a PWC
\alpha
-function satisfying (
5
).

For
V_{1},V_{2}\in\mathbb{F}(S_{B})
, we use the notation
V_{1}\leq V_{2}
if
V_{1}(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})\leq V_{2}(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})
for all
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})\in S_{B}
.
Since
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma\cup\{\alpha^{\star}\}
,
then it follows from Definition
6

that
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}\leq V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
.

In Algorithm
1
, if the backup at line 4 is executed, then the maxsup operator is applied to some states in
\phi
which may result in non-optimal minimax backup for other states in
\phi
, and if the backup at line 5 is executed,
\alpha^{\star}
is assigned the lower bound
L
over
\phi
. Therefore we have for any
(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})\in S_{B}
:

\displaystyle\langle\alpha^{\star},(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})\rangle

\displaystyle\leq[TV_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}](\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})

\displaystyle\leq[TV^{\star}](\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})

since
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}\leq V^{\star}

\displaystyle=V^{\star}(\hat{s}_{1},\hat{b}_{1})

by Theorem
4.2
.

(35)

Combining this inequality with
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}\leq V^{\star}
, we have
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\leq V^{\star}
as required.

The following lemma is required to prove the convergence of the algorithm.

Lemma 7
(Finite terminal belief points)

For any
t\geq 0
, if
\Psi_{t}\subseteq S_{B}
of belief points where the trials performed by the procedure
\mathit{Explore}
of Algorithm
2
terminated at exploration depth
t
, then
\Psi_{t}
is a finite set.

Proof

Consider any
t\geq 0
and suppose that
\Psi_{t}\subseteq S_{B}
is the set of belief points where the trials performed by the procedure
\mathit{Explore}
terminated at depth
t
.
In order to prove that
\Psi_{t}
is a finite set, we first need to show the following continuity of the lower and upper bounds. Using the same argument in the proof Theorem
4.1
, we can prove that the lower bound
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}
also has the continuity property of Theorem
4.1
, i.e., for any
(s_{1},b_{1}),(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})\in S_{B}
:

|V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{1},b_{1})-V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})|\leq K(b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})\,.

(36)

We still consider two beliefs
(s_{1},b_{1}),(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})\in S_{B}
. Let
(\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime})_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}
be the solution for
V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})
in (
3
), i.e.,

V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})=\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime}y_{i}+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1}^{\prime},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime}b_{1}^{i})\,.

(37)

Now since
(\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime})_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}
satisfies the constraints in (
3
) for
I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}
, it follows that:

\displaystyle V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})\leq\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime}y_{i}+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star}b_{1}^{i})

\displaystyle=\big(V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})-K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1}^{\prime},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star}b_{1}^{i})\big)+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime}b_{1}^{i})

by (
37
)

\displaystyle=V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})+\big(K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star\prime}b_{1}^{i})-K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1}^{\prime},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{i\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{i}^{\star}b_{1}^{i})\big)

rearranging

\displaystyle\leq V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})

by (
4
).

Using similar steps we can also show that:

V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})\leq V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})

and hence:

|V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})-V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})|\leq K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})\,.

(38)

Let a belief point
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})\in\Psi_{t}
. Since the procedure
\mathit{Explore}
terminates at
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
with exploration depth
t
, then the action-observation pair
(\hat{a}_{1},\hat{s}_{1})
computed by (
6
) (from line 7 of Algorithm
2
) satisfies

P(\hat{a}_{1},\hat{s}_{1}\mid(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t}),u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})\mathit{excess}_{t+1}(\hat{s}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},\hat{a}_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},\hat{s}_{1}})\leq 0\,.

Thus, for any
(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}
, if
P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t}),u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})>0
, then we have
\mathit{excess}_{t+1}(s_{1}^{\prime},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},a_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},s_{1}^{\prime}})\leq 0
, i.e.,

V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1}^{\prime},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},a_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},s_{1}^{\prime}})-V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{1}^{\prime},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},a_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},s_{1}^{\prime}})\leq\rho(t+1)\,.

(39)

Let
(u_{1}^{\mathit{lb}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})
and
(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{ub}})
be the minimax strategy profiles in stage games
[TV_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
and
[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
, respectively. Then, we denote by
J^{\mathit{lb}}(u_{1},u_{2})
and
J^{\mathit{ub}}(u_{1},u_{2})
the value of the stage game at
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
under the strategy pair
(u_{1},u_{2})\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})\times\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
when computing the backup values in (
22
) via
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}
and
V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}
, respectively. Thus, since
(u_{1}^{\mathit{lb}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})
is a minimax strategy profile:

\displaystyle J^{\mathit{lb}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})

\displaystyle\leq J^{\mathit{lb}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{lb}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})

\displaystyle=[TV_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})

by definition of
J^{\mathit{lb}}

\displaystyle\leq[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})

by Lemmas
2
and
3

\displaystyle=J^{\mathit{ub}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{ub}})

by definition of
J^{\mathit{ub}}

\displaystyle\leq J^{\mathit{ub}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})

(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{ub}})
is a minimax strategy profile.

(40)

Now using
(
40
) we have:

\displaystyle[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})-[TV_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}](s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})\leq J^{\mathit{ub}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})-J^{\mathit{lb}}(u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})

\displaystyle=\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in A_{1}\times S_{1}}$}P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t}),u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})

\displaystyle\quad(V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Gamma}(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},a_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},s_{1}^{\prime}})-V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s^{\prime}_{1},b_{1}^{s_{1}^{t},a_{1},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}},s_{1}^{\prime}}))

by (
22
)

\displaystyle\;\leq\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\in A_{1}\times S_{1}}$}P(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t}),u_{1}^{\mathit{ub}},u_{2}^{\mathit{lb}})\rho(t+1)

by (
39
)

\displaystyle=\beta\rho(t+1)

since
P
is a distribution.

(41)

Substituting (
41
) into the excess gap
\mathit{excess}_{t}(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
we have that the excess gap after performing the point-based update at
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})
in line 10 of Algorithm

:

\displaystyle\mathit{excess}_{t}(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})

\displaystyle\leq\beta\rho(t+1)-\rho(t)

\displaystyle=\rho(t)-2(U-L)\bar{\varepsilon}-\rho(t)

\displaystyle=-2(U-L)\bar{\varepsilon}

rearranging.

Due to the continuity (

) and (

), for any

, we have

(42)

Now, for every belief
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1})\in S_{B}

, substituting (

:

\displaystyle\mathit{excess}_{t}(s_{1}^{t},b_{1})\leq V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})-V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{1}^{t},b_{1}^{t})+2K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{t})-\rho(t)

\displaystyle\beta\rho(t+1)+2K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{t})-\rho(t)

by (

\displaystyle\leq\rho(t)-2(U-L)\bar{\varepsilon}+2K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{t})-\rho(t)

by definition of

\displaystyle\leq-2(U-L)\bar{\varepsilon}+2(U-L)\bar{\varepsilon}

since

\displaystyle=0

rearranging

which means that
(s_{1}^{t},b_{1})\notin\Psi_{t}
. Since
\mathbb{P}(S_{E})
is compact and thus totally bounded, we can conclude that
\Psi_{t}
is finite.

\sqcap
\sqcup

Proof
(Proof of Theorem
6.1
)

By the choice of
\bar{\varepsilon}
, the sequence
(\rho(t))_{t\in\mathbb{N}}
is monotonically increasing and unbounded. Since
L\leq V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{B})\leq V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{B})\leq U
for all
s_{B}\in S_{B}
, the difference between
V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}
and
V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}
is bounded by
U-L
. Therefore, there exists
T_{\max}
such that
\rho(T_{\max})\geq U-L\geq V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{B})-V_{\mathit{lb}}^{\Gamma}(s_{B})
for all
s_{B}\in S_{B}
, and therefore the recursive procedure
\mathit{Explore}
always terminates.

To demonstrate that Algorithm
2
terminates, we reason about the sets
\Psi_{t}\subseteq S_{B}
of belief points where the trials performed by the procedure
\mathit{Explore}
terminated at exploration depth
t
. Initially,
\Psi_{t}=\varnothing
for every
0\leq t<T_{max}
. Whenever the
\mathit{Explore}
recursion terminates at exploration depth
t
(i.e., the condition on line 9 does not hold), the belief
s_{B}^{t}
(which was the last belief considered during the trial) is added into the set
\Psi_{t}
, i.e.,
\Psi_{t}\triangleq\Psi_{t}\cup\{s_{B}^{t}\}
.
Since the agent state space
S_{1}
is finite and the number of possible termination depth is finite (
0\leq t<T_{max}
) and the set
\Psi_{t}
is finite by Lemma
7
, the algorithm has to terminate. Then, combining Lemmas
2
and
3
, the conclusion follows directly.

\sqcap
\sqcup

Lemma 8
(LP for upper bound)

For particle-based belief
(s_{1},b_{1})
, let
P(s_{E};b_{1})
be the probability of particle
s_{E}
under
b_{1}
. if the function
K_{\mathit{ub}}
equals
K
, i.e.,

K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},b_{1}^{\prime})=\mbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(U-L)\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{b_{1}(s_{E})+b_{1}^{\prime}(s_{E})>0}$}|P(s_{E};b_{1})-P(s_{E};b_{1}^{\prime})|

(43)

then
V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})
is the optimal value of the LP:

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm minimise}\;\;\mbox{$\sum_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}y_{k}+1/2(U-L)\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{s_{E}\in S_{E}^{+}}$}c_{s_{E}}\;\;\mbox{\rm subject to}

\displaystyle\quad c_{s_{E}}\geq|P(s_{E};b_{1})-\mbox{$\sum_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}P(s_{E};b_{1}^{k})|,\;\lambda_{k}\geq 0\;\;\mbox{and}\;\;\mbox{$\sum_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}=1

for
s_{E}\in S_{E}^{+}
and
k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}
, where

Proof

Theorem 0.D.2
(LP for minimax operator over upper bound, extended version of Theorem
6.2
)

For the function

, see (

), and particle-based belief

represented by

is the optimal value of the LP (

).

Proof

We first prove that given any
s_{1}\in S_{1}
,

is a convex function. Consider any two beliefs

\tau,\tau^{\prime}\geq 0
such that

. Let
(\lambda_{k}^{\star})_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}
and
(\lambda_{k}^{\prime\star})_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}
be optimal solutions of (
3
) for
V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})
and

respectively, i.e.,

\displaystyle V_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}(s_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}^{\star}y_{k}+K_{\mathit{ub}}(b_{1},\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}^{\star}b_{1}^{k})

E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle E$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle E$}}$}}}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

it follows that
J(u_{1},u_{2})
equals:

\displaystyle\mbox{$\min_{\overline{\nu},\overline{c}}$}\big(E_{1}+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}}$}p^{a_{1}}p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+C^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big)\big)

(52)

Now, given any
u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)
, let
\Lambda
be the feasible set for
(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})
, which is convex using (
51
). We then define a game with strategy spaces
\Lambda
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and payoff function
J_{u_{2}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\Lambda\times\mathbb{P}(A_{1})\to\mathbb{R}
which is the objective of (
52
), i.e., for
(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda
and
u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
:

\displaystyle J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),u_{1})=E_{1}+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}p^{a_{1}}p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+C^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big)\,.

(53)

Combining (
50
), (
52
) and (
53
) we have:

\displaystyle[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}J(u_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\mbox{$\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}$}J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),u_{1})\,.

(54)

We next show that the von Neumann’s Minimax Theorem
[
27
]
applies to the game with payoff function
J_{u_{2}}
and strategy spaces
\Lambda
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
. This theorem requires that:

•

\Lambda
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
are compact convex sets;

•

J_{u_{2}}
is a continuous function that is concave-convex, i.e.,
J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),\cdot)
is concave for fixed
(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})
and
J_{u_{2}}(\cdot,u_{1})
is convex for fixed
u_{1}
.

Clearly
\Lambda
and
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
are compact convex sets and by (
53
),
J_{u_{2}}
is bilinear in
\overline{\nu},\overline{c}
and
u_{1}
, and thus concave in
\mathbb{P}(A_{1})
and convex in
\Lambda
. Hence we can apply von Neumann’s Minimax Theorem, which gives us:

\begin{array}[]{c}\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),u_{1})=\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),u_{1})\,.\end{array}

Therefore, using this result and (
54
) we have that:

\displaystyle[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1},b_{1})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}$}\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}J_{u_{2}}((\overline{\nu},\overline{c}),u_{1})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}$}\mbox{$\max_{u_{1}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{1})}$}\big(E_{1}+

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}$}p^{a_{1}}p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+C^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big)\big)

by (
53
)

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\min_{u_{2}\in\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\mid S)}$}\mbox{$\min_{(\overline{\nu},\overline{c})\in\Lambda}$}\mbox{$\max_{a_{1}\in A_{1}}$}\big(E_{1}+

\displaystyle\quad+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+C^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big)\big)

where the final equality follows from the fact that, for fixed
u_{2}
and
\overline{\nu}
and
\overline{c}
, the objective is linear in
u_{1}
,
from which
[TV_{\mathit{ub}}^{\Upsilon}](s_{1},b_{1})

can be formulated as the LP problem given by minimise
v
subject to:

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\;\geq E_{1}+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big(\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+C^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\big)\big)\;\;\mbox{for all $a_{1}\in A_{1}$.}

(55)

Letting
\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
and
c_{s_{E}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}=p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}d_{s_{E}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
, we can reformulate (
55
) as minimise
v
subject to:

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\;\geq\mbox{$\sum_{i=1}^{n_{b}}\sum_{a_{2}}$}\kappa_{i}p_{a_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{E}^{i}}r((s_{1},s_{E}^{i}),(a_{1},a_{2}))+\beta\mbox{$\sum_{s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}}$}v_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\quad

\displaystyle v_{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;=\mbox{$\sum_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}y_{k}+\mbox{$\frac{1}{2}$}(U-L)\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}}$}\hat{c}_{s_{E}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}\quad.

for all
a_{1}\in A_{1}
and
s_{1}^{\prime}\in S_{1}
, where
u_{2}(a_{2}|s_{1},s_{E}^{i})=p_{a_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{E}^{i}}
.
We next compute the constraints for
\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
and
\hat{c}_{s_{E}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
. According to the belief update (

):

\displaystyle p^{a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}b_{1}^{s_{1},a_{1},u_{2},s_{1}^{\prime}}(s_{E}^{\prime})=P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})\frac{P(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})}{P(s_{1}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})}

\displaystyle=P(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime}\mid(s_{1},b_{1}),a_{1},u_{2})

rearranging

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\sum_{i=1}^{n_{b}}\sum_{a_{2}}$}\kappa_{i}p_{a_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{E}^{i}}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}^{i}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})\,

where the final equality follows from the definition of a particle-based belief.
Since
\nu_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}
and

are subject to the linear constraints (
51
), it follows that:

\displaystyle c_{s_{E}^{\prime}}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;\geq\Big|\mbox{$\sum_{i=1}^{n_{b}}\sum_{a_{2}}$}\kappa_{i}p_{a_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{E}^{i}}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}^{i}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\qquad-\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}P(s_{E}^{\prime};b_{1}^{k})\Big|

\displaystyle\mbox{$\sum\nolimits_{k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}}$}\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;=\mbox{$\sum_{i=1}^{n_{b}}\sum_{a_{2},s_{E}^{\prime}}$}\kappa_{i}p_{a_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{E}^{i}}\delta((s_{1},s_{E}^{i}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(s_{1}^{\prime},s_{E}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\;\geq 0

(56)

for all
(a_{1},s_{1}^{\prime})\in A_{1}\times S_{1}
,
1\leq i\leq n_{b}
and
s_{E}^{\prime}\in S_{E}
,
k\in I_{s^{\prime}_{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}{\scalebox{1}[.75]{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\displaystyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\textstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptstyle 1$}}{\scalebox{.75}[1]{$\scriptscriptstyle 1$}}$}}}}
.
Thus, the optimization problem can be reformulated as the LP problem in (

).

Figure 4
:
Pedestrian-vehicle interaction: local transition diagram over the local states, i.e., vehicle speeds (

(

Finally, we give some additional details and statistics for the models
developed for the two case studies used for evaluation in Section
7
.

Pedestrian-vehicle interaction.

The one-sided NS-POSG for the pedestrian-vehicle scenario is defined as follows:

•

S_{1}=\mathit{Loc}_{1}{\times}\mathit{Per}_{1}
, where:

\displaystyle\mathit{Loc}_{1}

\displaystyle=\;\{30,27,24,21,18,15,12,9,6,3,0\}

\displaystyle\mathit{Per}_{1}

\displaystyle=\;\{``\emph{unlikelytocross}",``\emph{likelytocross}",``\emph{verylikelytocross}"\}

are the vehicle’s discrete speeds (km/h) and perceived pedestrian intentions, respectively.

•

S_{E}=\{((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))\in(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}\mid(0\leq x_{1},x_{2}\leq 20)\wedge(0\leq y_{1},y_{2}\leq 10)\}
, where
(x_{1},y_{1})
and
(x_{2},y_{2})
are the top-left coordinates of the 2D fixed-size bounding boxes of size
0.5{\times}1.5\;(\textup{m}^{2})

around the pedestrian at the previous and current steps, respectively.

•

A=A_{1}{\times}A_{2}
, where
A_{1}=\{-3,0,3\}
(
\textup{m/s}^{2}
) are the possible accelerations of the vehicle, and
A_{2}=\{\mathit{cross},\mathit{back}\}
are the possible directions the pedestrian can choose to move.

•

For each local state
v_{1}\in\mathit{Loc}_{1}
and environment state
((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))\in S_{E}
, we let
\mathit{obs}_{1}(v_{1},((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})))=f^{\max}_{\mathit{ped}}((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))
, where
f_{\mathit{ped}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}S_{E}\to\mathbb{P}(\mathit{Per}_{1})
is a data-driven pedestrian intention classifier implemented via a feed-forward NN with ReLU activation functions and trained over the PIE dataset in
[
29
]
. Note here
\mathit{obs}_{1}
is independent of the local state of
\mathsf{Ag}_{1}
.

•

For
(v_{1},\mathit{per}_{1})\in\mathit{Loc}_{1}{\times}\mathit{Per}_{1}
,
v_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathit{Loc}_{1}
and
(a_{1},a_{2})\in A
we have:

\delta_{1}((v_{1},\mathit{per}_{1}),(a_{1},a_{2}))(v_{1}^{\prime})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\mbox{if $v_{1}^{\prime}=g_{\mathit{next}}(v_{1},a_{1})$}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

where
g_{\mathit{next}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathit{Loc}_{1}\times A_{1}\to\mathit{Loc}_{1}
is the speed update function of the vehicle with the transition diagram in Fig.
4
.

•

For
v_{1}\in\mathit{Loc}_{1}
,
((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})),((x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime}),(x_{2}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}))\in S_{E}
and
(a_{1},a_{2})\in A
we have

\delta_{E}(v_{1},((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2})),(a_{1},a_{2}))((x_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}^{\prime}),(x_{2}^{\prime},y_{2}^{\prime}))=1
where

\displaystyle x_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=x_{2},

\displaystyle y_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=y_{2},

\displaystyle x_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=x_{2}+\mathit{move}(a_{2})v_{2}\Delta t,

\displaystyle y_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=y_{2}-v_{1}\Delta t-\frac{a_{1}}{2}{\Delta t}^{2},

v_{2}=4.5
(
m/s
) is the speed of the pedestrian,
\mathit{move}(a_{2})
is the direction of the movement of the pedestrian action, i.e.,
\mathit{move}(\mathit{cross}){=}-1
and
\mathit{move}(\mathit{back}){=}1
, and
\Delta t=|g_{\mathit{next}}(v_{1},a_{1})-v_{1}|/|a_{1}|
if
a_{1}\neq 0
and
0.3
(
s
) otherwise.

A crash occurs if the environment state is in the set:

\mathcal{R}_{\mathit{crash}}=\{((x_{1},y_{1}),(x_{2},y_{2}))\in S_{E}\mid(0\leq x_{2}\leq 0.5)\wedge(0\leq y_{2}\leq 2.5)\}

i.e., the current bounding box around the pedestrian has a distance of no more than
0.5
and
1.0
(
m
) along the
x
and
y
coordinates
to the vehicle, respectively (recall the bounding box has size
0.5{\times}1.5\;(\textup{m}^{2}
)).
The reward structure is such that, for any
(s_{1},s_{E})\in S
and
a\in A
,
r((s_{1},s_{E}),a)=-200
if
s_{E}\in\mathcal{R}_{\mathit{crash}}
and
0
otherwise. To model a scenario where the pedestrian and the vehicle require different update frequencies, we can make the following adjustments:
1)
select the time step of the model as that corresponding to the larger frequency;
2)
augment the state with the current time step and the last taken action of the agent with smaller frequency;
3)
build the environment transition function such that the last taken action of the agent with smaller frequency is used for the update within the lower frequency, which is tracked by the current time step.

Pursuit-evasion game.

We modify the example presented in
[
19
]
by
considering a continuous environment
\mathcal{R}\triangleq\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\mid 0\leq x,y\leq 3\}
that is partitioned into multiple cells by perception functions. In this game, we have a pursuer agent
\mathsf{Ag}_{p}

that tries to catch an evader agent
\mathsf{Ag}_{e}
. In each step, the evader moves by picking from the set of actions
A_{e}\triangleq\{\textit{up},\textit{down},\textit{left},\textit{right}\}
. The pursuer moves in a similar manner with additional diagonal movements, and thus has the action set
A_{p}\triangleq\{\textit{up},\textit{down},\textit{left},\textit{right},\textit{upleft},\textit{upright},\textit{downleft},\textit{downright}\}
.

The evader has full observation and knows the exact location of both players. The pursuer has partial observation, that is, it knows which cell it is in, but does not know its exact location and does not know which cell the evader is in. The perception function of the pursuer employs an NN classifier
f_{\mathcal{R}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathit{Grid})
, where
\mathit{Grid}\triangleq\{(i,j)\mid 1\leq i,j\leq 3\}
, which takes the location (coordinates) of the pursuer as input and outputs a probability distribution over nine abstract grid points (cells), thus partitioning the environment as illustrated by Fig.
5
. This is modelled as a one-sided NS-POSG as follows.

Figure 5
:
Representation of a regular (left) and coarse (right) perception function for the pursuit-evasion example. Each graph depicts the boundaries of the nine abstract grid cells learnt by the classifiers. The pre-images of the regular and coarse perception functions are composed of 48 and 50 polytopes, respectively.

•

S_{1}=\mathit{Loc}_{1}{\times}Per_{1}
, where the local state
Loc_{1}=\{\bot\}
is a dummy state and
\mathit{Per}_{1}=\mathit{Grid}
.

•

S_{E}=\{((x_{p},y_{p}),(x_{e},y_{e}))\in\mathcal{R}^{2}\}
.

•

A=A_{1}{\times}A_{2}
, where
A_{1}=A_{p}
and
A_{2}=A_{e}
.

•

\mathit{obs}_{i}(\bot,(x_{p},y_{p}))=f_{\mathcal{R}}^{\max}(x_{p},y_{p})
and the NN classifier
f_{\mathcal{R}}\mathrel{\mathop{\mathchar 58\relax}}\mathcal{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathit{Grid})
is implemented via a feed-forward NN with one hidden ReLU layer and 14 neurons.

•

For environment states
s_{E}=((x_{p},y_{p}),(x_{e},y_{e}))
,
s_{E}^{\prime}=((x_{p}^{\prime},y_{p}^{\prime}),(x_{e}^{\prime},y_{e}^{\prime}))
, local state
\mathit{loc}_{1}
and joint action
a=(a_{1},a_{2})
:

\delta_{E}(\bot,s_{E},a)(s_{E}^{\prime})=\mbox{$\prod_{i\in\{p,e\}}$}\delta_{E_{i}}((x_{i},y_{i}),a_{i})(x_{i}^{\prime},y_{i}^{\prime})

where for
i\in\{p,e\}
:

\delta_{E_{i}}((x_{i},y_{i}),a_{i})(x^{\prime}_{i},y^{\prime}_{i})=\\
\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}1&\mbox{if $x_{i}^{\prime}=x_{i}+d_{a_{i}}^{x}$ and $y^{\prime}_{i}=y_{i}+d_{a_{i}}^{y}$}\\
0&\mbox{otherwise}\end{array}\right.

and the pairs
(d_{a_{i}}^{x},d_{a_{i}}^{y})
indicate the direction of movement under action
a_{i}
, e.g.,
(d_{\mathit{up}}^{x},d_{\mathit{up}}^{y})=(0,1)
and
(d_{\mathit{left}}^{x},d_{\mathit{left}}^{y})=(-1,0)
.

The capture condition in
[
19
]
is also used, that is, the evader is captured if it is in the same cell as the pursuer, so the set of capture states
\mathcal{R}_{\mathit{capture}}
is given by:

\{((x_{p},y_{p}),(x_{e},y_{e}))\in S_{E}\mid\,\exists(i,j)\in\mathit{Grid},\,(i{-}1{\leq}x_{p},x_{e}{<}i)\wedge(j{-}1{\leq}y_{p},y_{e}{<}j)\}\,.

Unlike
[
19
]
, the game does not end once the evader is captured, yielding the possibility of multiple captures. In case the pursuer is successful, that is, it enters the same cell as the evader, it receives a reward of 100. This can be modelled by assigning that value to any state-action pair with the state in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathit{capture}}
.

A model where the pursuer agent
\mathsf{Ag}_{p}
has two pursuers under its control was also developed. For that model, the actions available to the pursuer agent are pairs corresponding to chosen directions of the pursuers, where they can now only move horizontally or vertically, i.e., the actions available to the pursuer agent are given by
A_{p}\triangleq(\{\textit{up},\textit{down},\textit{left},\textit{right}\})^{2}
. Additionally, the perception function of the pursuer agent is modified to take the coordinates of both pursuers and output the perceived grid cell for each of them. In this scenario, a capture happens if the evader is in the same grid cell as either pursuer.

Impact of perception.
In Fig.
2
(b), when the pursuer starts from cell E (marked by the red dot), its perception function incorrectly observes that it is in cell G (denoted by the blue border). In the next step, when it is in fact in cell B, the perception function shows it to be in cell A. As the pursuer’s actions are tied to where it perceives itself to be
(e.g., in cell A it cannot use left or down actions to avoid moving out of bounds),
the number of explored regions is reduced. Hence, the evader can safely hide in different portions of the environment. In particular, we see that it moves from its initial position to cells A or I and stays there, as those cells would not be visited by the pursuer. The pursuer does not actually capture the evader even if its perceived location cell almost completely coincides with a green area that the evader is highly likely to stay in.

Statistics.

Table
1
shows statistics for solving various instances,
varying the number of points in the initial belief and discount factor
\beta
.
The table presents
the initial and final values of the upper and lower bounds,
the number of
\alpha
-functions generated for the lower bound computation (
|\Gamma|
),
the number of belief points for the upper bound computation (
|\Upsilon|
), and the number of iterations and the time required. In the experiments, we have set a timeout of 120 minutes (except for the first row where the timeout was reduced to 15 minutes). In addition, Fig.
6
shows how the lower and upper bound values change for the initial belief as the number of iterations increases for one instance of the pursuit-evasion game.

Since our algorithm is
anytime
, lower and upper bounds hold throughout computations and we successfully generate meaningful strategies
(discussed further below) on a range of models.
However, computation is generally slow due to the number of LP problems to solve (whose size increases with
|\Gamma|
and
|\Upsilon|
), as well as expensive operations over polyhedra and the probabilistic branching of mixed strategies to guide exploration.

Both Table
1
and Fig.
6
illustrate the impact of these factors. In the first two rows of Table
1
we observe the difference between a 15 and 120 minute timeout for the same instance of pursuit-evasion game with a single pursuer. As can be seen, the increase in the timeout causes the lower bound to improve (increase) by 0.2145, while the upper bound improves (decreases) by 2.55. With a timeout of 15 minutes we see that 169 iterations are performed; however, due to the number of
\alpha
-functions growing from 184 to 515, increasing the timeout to 120 minutes only allows 95 more iterations to be performed.

Considering Fig.
6
, we initially see a sharp decrease of the upper bound, but improvement to either bound becomes progressively harder as computation progresses. The entry for the pursuit-evasion game with a single pursuer with a coarser perception function in Table
1
(highlighted in bold) is the only instance that converges before the timeout due to the fact that the number of reachable regions is smaller.

Table
1
also shows that, as expected, larger discount factors lead to larger lower and upper bounds. For the pursuit-evasion model with two pursuers, the larger difference between the bound values reached at the timeout is a consequence of a higher branching factor during exploration slowing down the computation, as we have 64 joint actions in each state. For the entries related to the pedestrian-vehicle interaction model in the table, the final upper bound values match their initial values due to the fact that the initial beliefs were selected so that it should be possible to avoid a crash if an optimal strategy was played.

We note that HSVI for
finite
one-sided POSGs, in
[
19
]
,
is already computationally very expensive, even with multiple optimisations
(
[
19
]
uses a timeout of 10 hours, versus 2 hours here).
